## Visual and technical audit

**Current technical result:** the laptop can produce and load SPZ from real chair footage.  
**Current visual result:** all seven published opening previews fail as chair inspection views. Golden is hazy, Repeat and several variants look like small distorted fragments, and the clean source stills are more recognizable. The actual underlying asset may look different from another viewpoint. The offline experiments examined Golden and the 10.03-second stress take under corrected framing. They support a viewer bug plus a genuine asset-quality problem.

| Finding | Evidence and implication |
|---|---|
| Misapplied centering after rotation | `src/scan-viewer.js` rotates the splat 180° around X but subtracts the unrotated local center. A synthetic nonzero center `(0.3, 1.2, −0.8)` mapped to `(0, −2.4, 1.6)` under the current transform and `(0, 0, 0)` with the corrected transform. A corrected Golden render moved the scene while haze remained. Fix this independently of training. |
| Scene bounds dominate the camera | The current fit uses the bounding-box diagonal of all splat centers and Spark's free-camera controls. Outlier removal in the fit diagnostic made the chair larger in some views, but a percentile box still includes room geometry. Build a reliable subject pivot, orbit, reset and resize behavior. Track framing changes separately from artifact changes. |
| Outliers are measured in the SPZ data | All seven SPZs decoded, counts matched and position/scale columns were finite. Full bounds diagonals are 1.54–2.83× the axis-wise 1st–99th percentile diagonals. Across the takes the largest longest-axis scale is 70.1–202.9× its take's median. This identifies outlier sensitivity without labelling any individual splat as chair or room. |
| Simple trimming helps and remains insufficient | At matched diagnostic views, removing about the largest 1% by longest axis removed 270 Golden splats and 317 stress-take splats. Some haze and streaks receded. An oblique Golden view still showed a cupboard and the stress take still showed floor/room fragments. The trim was applied only to scratch copies. It changes the encoded bytes, so use a no-op round-trip control in later visual comparisons. |
| Apparent downscaling is misdescribed | The worker's `scale=1280:-2` fixes width. All 21 published portrait stills are **1280 × 2276**, while job metadata records encoded landscape dimensions. The old “1280 long edge” description cannot certify the actual training set. Check rotation, actual written dimensions, masks and camera intrinsics together when changing the resolution. Recalibrate whenever the frame dimensions change. |
| Existing QA is a readiness gate | WebGL readiness, positive splat count, PNG bytes and center/edge pixels confirm something rendered. They all tolerated visually poor scans here. The supplied 1440 px capture of the 10.03 s take has a viewer about 684 × 513 px. The main high-contrast content spans about 123 × 108 px including artifacts, about 1.17% of canvas pixels under the diagnostic threshold. True quality needs matched views and independent examination. |
| Replay can look like a new scan | The batch harness uses a filename title, fixture 60 × 60 × 100 cm dimensions, and `clip study` device label. The preview uses fresh-recording language and a completed checklist. Identify the result as a replay, separate measurements from fixture data and let the source frames substantiate provenance. |
| Timing needs a proper boundary | Across the seven runs the reported worker-stage sum spent ~45% in alignment and ~54% training. The median conversion was ~0.52 s. Reported totals exclude capture, upload, queue and first usable render. The lazy viewer JS was ~959 kB gzip before downloading the SPZ. Time training and alignment first after passing the quality gate, and measure loading independently. |

The audit instructions, per-take stats, and contact sheets are in the approved ZIP. The repository and published evidence stayed untouched during the audit. No later quality winner was reported in this session.

## Overnight execution and owner decisions

The owner requested an autonomous overnight session for **DSH with DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017 on max effort**. They explicitly approved creating the ZIP after the audit. They chose quality first and speed second, an opt-in winning preset, and full focus on 3D quality. They declined the proposed photo-only fallback work. Use source stills for diagnosis and reference. Keep local segmentation downloads allowed if a mask experiment is justified. Use current pinned tools first, with one GPU task at a time on the RTX 4060. Independent code, QA and review tasks can run in parallel through DSH subagents.

**ZIP start:** extract at the existing project root. The working path observed is `C:\Users\fauzi\OneDrive\Documents\GitHub\Lihatdulu\`. The top-level added folder is `overnight-scan\`. In a new DSH session opened in the project, choose the stated model and effort, then paste `overnight-scan\PASTE_INTO_DSH.md`. The detailed prompt and core instructions are preserved verbatim in the appendices below.

### Overnight procedure and gates

1. **Preflight and isolate.** Keep the primary checkout and original clips intact. Verify source and evidence commits, local versions, and disk. Use a local worktree on `work/scan-quality-overnight-<id>`, and a distinct loopback port and `SCAN_ROOT` inside the run. Keep old job schemas readable. Preserve usable current output before pruning. If the raw clips or GPU tools are missing, continue viewer and evidence work with the published SPZs.
2. **Baseline and viewer.** Compare the same SPZ with old camera, corrected center and outcome-driven framing. Check a stable chair-centered orbit at desktop and narrow widths, reset, zoom, resize, route lifecycle, and real views. A larger blob is insufficient.
3. **Low-cost artifact trials and object-focused reconstruction.** Evidence points to room leakage and large streaks. Test conservative filtering on copies with fixed view and no-op control. If the chair still fails, preserve/rebuild one aligned set, evaluate independently inspected object masks after alignment under the actual Brush mask convention, and compare 3,000 versus 6,000 steps on the same aligned dataset. Only try 9,000 if the earlier increase produces a visual gain and there is time. Measure blur, exposure, framing, angular coverage and actual output size before changing extraction or calibration. Keep each change attributable.
4. **Freeze then confirm.** Tune on Golden, freeze the candidate, run Repeat as the held-out confirmation, and report the 10.03-second stress result independently. Compare front/three-quarter, side, rear and detail where available, plus an orbit and source stills. Look for complete recognizable chair shape and thin features, manageable clutter, and no added damage to the back, arms, legs and wheels. Translate numerical checks into visual evidence. If no candidate clears both Golden and Repeat, preserve the best diagnostic and say which specific features still fail.
5. **Speed and integration.** Start speed work after the quality pass. Use matched timing and compare a smaller real long edge or fewer steps one at a time. Keep the fastest option that meets the visual floor. A passing option is opt-in with exact launch command, provenance and rollback to the existing default. Local replay can exercise upload, worker, status, preview, and reload without new recording. Preserve the separate Sora demo and check adjacent marketplace behavior.
6. **Budget and morning output.** Six hours total with 40 minutes reserved for verification and handoff. One coordinator owns decision history, run checkpoints, recovery and integration. Keep experiments in `var/overnight/<run-id>/` with `status.json`, `preflight.txt`, `experiments.jsonl`, `comparisons/`, `best/`, `logs/`, `qa/`, and `MORNING.md`. Write the run path to `var/overnight/LATEST.txt` in the original checkout. `MORNING.md` opens with the best local result and exact command, matched renders, measured timing, branch/commit and test outcomes, and remaining phone work. Keep code changes and evidence local.

The ZIP checks verified the top-level layout, valid archive and extraction, working Markdown links, and 49 pinned per-take evidence artifacts through the extracted preflight. That ran in the local Linux audit environment. Full DSH orchestration, the Windows/Helios binaries and a six-hour reconstruction run were not executed here. The separate final paste-ready DSH prompt delivered after the ZIP appears below.

### Direct DSH prompt for a new session

```text
Run the LihatDulu overnight 3D scan improvement project end to end. Start by reading AGENTS.md and overnight-scan/START_HERE.md, then read AUDIT.md, RUNBOOK.md, EXPERIMENTS.json, ACCEPTANCE.md, and MORNING_TEMPLATE.md in that folder. Execute the runbook in this session.

The goal is the best inspectable 3D chair the existing footage can support. Quality first, speed second. Use the existing recordings and SPZs. Skip photo-only fallback work. Fix the confirmed viewer defects, compare reconstruction candidates using matched camera views, and inspect the actual renders. Use Golden for tuning, Repeat to confirm the frozen winner, and the 10.03-second take as a stress case. Treat registration, splat count, and a ready status as diagnostics. The chair needs to look convincing from multiple angles.

Act as coordinator. Delegate independent viewer, pipeline, review, and regression tasks to subagents where useful. Use a single runner for GPU work on the RTX 4060. Work in a separate local Git worktree and keep the primary checkout, original recordings, and existing scan jobs intact. Keep the current default available and make any validated winner an opt-in preset. Keep changes and evidence local.

Run for up to six hours, reserving the last 40 minutes for verification and handoff. Start with preflight, create a run folder under var/overnight/, and write its path to var/overnight/LATEST.txt. Save a checkpoint after each meaningful experiment. If one path is blocked, continue with the existing SPZs or another independent task.

Finish with MORNING.md. Put the best result and exact command to open it first, followed by matched before-and-after renders, measured timings, the local branch and commit, test results, and what still needs a physical-phone check. If no candidate meets the visual gate, show the strongest result and explain what still fails.

Begin now.
```

# LihatDulu overnight scan quality run

## Start on the Helios

1. Extract the ZIP into the LihatDulu checkout that contains `package.json` and `AGENTS.md`. The expected location is `C:\Users\fauzi\OneDrive\Documents\GitHub\Lihatdulu\`. The archive adds the single folder `overnight-scan\`.

Give DSH access to the project workspace and pinned local tools. For the six-hour run, leave the laptop plugged in and awake.
3. In the morning, open `var\overnight\LATEST.txt` in the original checkout and then `MORNING.md` in the run directory it names. The report links to the comparison images, viewer or local command, Git branch, and actual winning result when one passes.

## What comes with the run

Run the LihatDulu overnight 3D scan improvement project end to end. Use the current DSH session and max-effort model setting. Read `AGENTS.md`, then `overnight-scan/AUDIT.md`, `RUNBOOK.md`, `EXPERIMENTS.json`, `ACCEPTANCE.md`, and `MORNING_TEMPLATE.md`. The runbook and current owner decisions govern this task.

Quality comes first. Produce the strongest inspectable 3D chair the existing footage supports, then optimize the parts of the winning setup that cost time. Skip photo-only fallback development.

Act as the coordinator. Use parallel subagents for independent viewer development, review, diagnostics, and regression work when the harness supports them. Give each a bounded path and concrete proof to return. Use a single GPU runner for reconstruction, segmentation, and GPU-heavy rendering. Keep one run ledger and one final integration owner. Work in a separate local worktree, retain existing footage and job files, and keep all outputs local. Run for up to six hours with the final 40 minutes reserved for independent review, verification, and handoff. Proceed through recoverable blockers and save completed work on a local branch. Treat interruptions and environmental limits as facts to record in the ledger.

Grade visual usefulness against actual reference stills and fixed rendered views, with the same camera for an A/B comparison, plus an orbit check. Review the actual images. Begin executing. Continue until the time limit or the runbook's completion condition, whichever arrives first.

### ZIP snapshot RUNBOOK.md

````text
# Overnight execution runbook

## Owner decisions

Prioritize 3D quality, then speed. Integrate an evidence-backed winner as an opt-in preset. Use the existing phone footage and the RTX 4060 laptop. Project-local downloads for an open segmentation tool and model are authorized if needed for the mask trial. Record tool versions and download origins. Preserve the Vite, JavaScript, Three.js, SparkJS, and local reconstruction setup. Keep the prepared Sora demo separate. Keep branch changes and run evidence local.

## 0. Set up and checkpoint

1. Read the project `AGENTS.md` and the current scan handoff. Collect `git status --short --branch` and commit IDs. Record the start time and a deadline six hours later. Create `var/overnight/<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>/` in the original checkout, write its absolute path to `var/overnight/LATEST.txt`, and save the preflight output. Use one durable ledger per run.
2. Keep the current checkout and branch in place. Fetch `origin`, verify the evidence commit, and create a sibling worktree on a new local `work/scan-quality-overnight-<id>` branch from current `origin/main`, after checking that it contains the audited baseline `a26d952`. If the base has advanced, note which audited lines changed and reproduce each defect on the new base. A detached evidence worktree at `cbf69f09fbb41eacaa8ea40e6e6cb55ceec61e4c` is a convenient source of the seven pinned jobs. Keep test builds and repository edits in the implementation worktree.
3. Run `node overnight-scan/tools/preflight.mjs` from the original checkout after the fetch. Use `--evidence <evidence-worktree>` to verify evidence bytes by hash. Use `--clips <directory>` if the originals moved. Record actual FFmpeg/ffprobe, COLMAP, Brush, Node and splat-transform versions. Check actual help before passing experimental flags. Run the project doctor, install the lockfile in the implementation worktree, and capture baseline unit, build, marketplace, and simulated-scan results. A baseline failure goes in the ledger with its exact environment.
4. Resolve clips from the original checkout's `var/spike/clips` or an explicit override. Record input SHA-256 for actual training runs. Locate `var/tools/colmap/bin/colmap.exe` and `var/tools/brush/brush_app.exe` in the original checkout. The scan worker accepts `SCAN_COLMAP` and `SCAN_BRUSH` as absolute paths. Use `node node_modules/@playcanvas/splat-transform/bin/cli.mjs --help` from the implementation worktree for the pinned converter.
5. Keep the evidence and inputs read-only. Start all test servers on loopback with a distinct `SCAN_ROOT` inside the run directory, a fresh build via `SCAN_DIST` if needed, and a free `SCAN_PORT`. Set retention high enough for the isolated run or copy accepted jobs to a separate evidence directory before they can be pruned. The server prunes completed jobs at startup and after runs. Never target the primary `var/scans` for a benchmark. Set `QA_OUTPUT` per suite to keep its screenshots in the run.
6. Reserve the last 40 minutes for review and handoff. Start a GPU run only if its timeout and the remaining verification work fit. Begin with at least 20 GiB free if doing fresh reconstructions and keep an 8 GiB reserve. If available space falls below the reserve, finish artifact audit and code QA with existing inputs. Log unexpected GPU adapters, OOM, or thermal throttling when observed.

## 1. Split work by ownership

| Owner | Job | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Coordinator | Deadline, experiment queue, comparison protocol, integrations, ledger, morning report | One decision per candidate, reproducible paths and commands |
| Viewer agent | Camera transform, robust fit, explicit object orbit and reset, resize and disposal | Same-SPZ A/B shots and orbit recording at desktop and mobile widths |
| Pipeline agent | Orientation and size provenance, staged benchmark, profile options, replay checks | Exact input hashes, versioned configs, stage timings, job identity, isolated outputs |
| Evidence reviewer | Source stills, qualitative grading, holdout and stress comparisons | Actual image review and explicit failures, preferably blinded to config |
| Regression agent | Test matrix and independent build checks | Unit, marketplace, simulated scan, optional real-file replay and known limitations |

The coordinator holds one GPU lease. Mask generation on GPU, COLMAP/Brush, GPU filtering, and GPU rendering share it. CPU asset analysis, code edits in disjoint paths, reading, and review can run in parallel. If the harness supports fewer agents, combine roles while retaining a separate review pass. Maintain enough context in `status.json` to resume without relying on a conversation transcript.

## 2. Highest-leverage sequence

### A. Lock the comparison and repair the viewer

Start with the existing SPZs. Use Golden for tuning, the 10.03-second take as a stress case, and Repeat for confirmation once a candidate is frozen. Establish repeatable views from documented camera poses. If original camera matrices are available after a re-run, use approximate source-camera views to check appearance and an orbit to test inspection. Record how angles were chosen. Compare camera and asset changes separately.

1. Reproduce the local-to-world centering issue on a synthetic nonzero-center fixture, then on the real files. Correct the transform with the actual applied orientation. Confirm axis conventions and an upright look against the reference photos.
2. Evaluate a robust bounds diagnostic and a subject-specific center or region of interest. Global percentiles help identify distant splats and can still fit room geometry. Keep a stable fitted view, subject-centered orbit, reset, bounded zoom, and a view that survives resize and route changes. Keep comparison camera parameters constant when testing assets.
3. Evaluate an asset cleanup on copies. Export a no-op round trip and verify count, pose, and appearance. Compare conservative scale-based filtering, then an object-region or isolated-floater trial if clear evidence supports it. Render before and after with the same camera. Pay attention to the mesh behind the backrest, arm supports, chair legs and wheels, and any holes introduced by filtering.
4. Prepare three views for every candidate considered for promotion and an orbit sample for finalists. Compare outputs visually to the stills. A pass in the old screenshot harness means the asset loaded. Keep renderer readiness, visual usefulness, and physical-phone verification as separate evidence.

### B. Improve reconstruction only where the evidence points

1. Re-run the unchanged baseline on Golden if the camera dataset is missing or unusable. Retain the extracted frames and chosen sparse COLMAP model inside the isolated run before the worker's normal cleanup. Record frame count, actual written dimensions, rotation from the input when available, alignment version, and profile values. Use checksummed immutable dataset copies or read-only reuse for comparisons.
2. Run the `Q1` and `Q2` candidates from the matrix in evidence-driven order. Use fixed alignment for single-variable changes to Brush training steps or training masks. For optional segmentation, use an official project source and local inference. Verify the installed Brush mask convention and a small sample. Manually inspect masks on front, side and rear frames, including mesh gaps, arms, and wheel silhouettes before launching a full run.
3. Evaluate capture coverage and orientation when faster tests fail to recover shape. Inspect registered camera positions across the orbit, timestamps, frame sharpness, exposure, and duplication near the start and end. Try one approved change to sampling or orientation-aware resolution at a time and rerun calibration for resized frames. Compare actual written dimensions. Do not increase the input frame count solely to satisfy a count. Keep all existing acceptance thresholds during experiments.
4. Freeze the most promising profile and apply it to Repeat, then review the stress case independently. If Repeat fails, downgrade the candidate and identify why before picking another one. Keep candidates separately labeled. Continue exploring a later camera or mask candidate if there is enough time for independent validation.

### C. Optimize the winning quality result

Only enter this step after a visual gate pass on Golden and Repeat. Reuse alignment when measuring training changes. Try a lower real long-edge target or a lower training budget independently, holding other settings fixed. Keep the fastest profile that clears the same visual floor. Report wall-clock capture-to-first-usable-view separately from recorded worker-stage totals. Include upload and queue time for local replay and measure viewer transfer/decode separately. Compare medians from repeated fast timings when time allows. Keep 3D quality as the priority even if the final runtime is higher.

### D. Integrate an opt-in candidate and verify

Choose a minimal entry point that makes the preset easy to try locally while retaining the existing default. Prefer a server-side opt-in config or CLI argument for the experiment. Include the selected profile in local provenance so it is reproducible. Keep old jobs readable. Existing job records validate exact keys, so an audit sidecar is often simpler than replacing the stored schema. Preserve normal camera permission behavior and do not change the separate prepared Sora route.

When time allows, replay one existing original through the local upload, processing and same-site preview flow. Record the input hash, resulting job ID, output ID, time to first usable view, and reload behavior. Label it as a replay. Keep validation for the actual phone, browser recording, and HTTPS tunnel on the morning checklist. Run unit, build, targeted browser, marketplace and simulated scan suites where applicable. Report a named check as passed only when observed. If an experimental resize profile or viewer change misses the gate, preserve its finding and leave the validated path available.

## 3. Self-review and stopping rules

Suggested checkpoints are 30 minutes for setup, 90 minutes for viewer diagnosis, 210 minutes for quality experimentation, 320 minutes for confirmation and possible speed work, and 360 minutes for the final handoff. Move time between phases based on evidence. Use the final 40 minutes for regression, comparison, and a readable report.

After each candidate, write the hypothesis, baseline, exact one-variable change, input hash, command, wall time, outputs, and actual findings to `experiments.jsonl`. Save failures with the exact code and logs. Give the reviewer source images and matched views, ask what became clearer and what regressed, then change the next experiment accordingly. Two inconclusive runs using the same strategy are a reason to move to another hypothesis. Avoid arbitrary repeated improvements to a metric that failed to predict a visible chair. If work is blocked, record the missing dependency and continue independent tasks using existing SPZs.

For timeout, OOM, or an interruption, checkpoint first and stop only processes owned by the run. Restart with details from the ledger and a fresh isolated output path. Keep partial runs separate from completed candidates. Protect the best observed candidate and its artifacts before cleanup. Keep final images, timing rows, settings, log excerpts, and the exact commands needed to open the result. Build `MORNING.md` from the template even if quality never passes. Record run-specific decisions, checked results, and the remaining milestone in the local worktree's handoff notes as appropriate.

## Output layout

```
var/overnight/
  LATEST.txt
  <run-id>/
    MORNING.md
    status.json
    preflight.txt
    experiments.jsonl
    datasets/
    jobs/
    comparisons/
    best/
    qa/
    logs/
```

`MORNING.md` should name the opt-in profile and the exact command or local path to open the best result, the worktree and commit, and what data is required to reproduce it. Keep the baseline and selected candidate available together.
````

### ZIP snapshot ACCEPTANCE.md

```text
# Acceptance and comparison protocol

Assess the result at the same viewport size and camera in each A/B pair. Save the actual images, not just data attributes or screenshot bytes. Use Golden to tune, Repeat to verify after values are frozen, and the 10.03-second take to see whether changes help poor footage. Use extra takes to check whether apparent gains generalize across the available chair evidence.

## Visual quality gate

| Check | Pass condition |
|---|---|
| Initial desktop and phone-width view | The whole chair is immediately recognizable and upright. It is centered, comfortably framed, and clearly separated from unrelated room fragments. |
| Three inspection views | Front or three-quarter, side, and rear views show consistent chair geometry. The reviewer can identify the seat, backrest, arm supports, and base or wheels where visible in the corresponding evidence. |
| Detail retention | The change preserves visible edges and materials, especially the thin frame and mesh. Visible holes, duplicate parts, haze, or foreground streaks do not hide the features needed to judge the chair. |
| Orbit and recovery | A drag changes the viewing angle around the subject. Zoom and reset keep the subject recoverable. Rotation is checked on a desktop viewport and a touch-emulated narrow viewport. Resize and route re-entry behave predictably. |
| Before and after | The reference stills and camera-matched baseline are available beside the candidate. Differences are reviewed in the actual frames. The reviewer can name concrete defects that improved and any tradeoffs. |
| Confirmation | Golden and the reserved Repeat both clear the floor with the same frozen config. Give the stress case its own demonstrated verdict. |

A passing visual result shows more than a deposit of pixels near the center. Grade each check `pass`, `partial`, `fail`, or `not observed`, with an image or video link and one sentence of evidence. Independently review finalists where possible. A headless screenshot gate or a high registration rate is evidence of execution. Treat a visual pass as an additional decision.

Use fixed views for diagnosis and fair comparison. If source camera extrinsics can be recovered, use an approximate source camera view in addition to a novel orbit. Pixels from mismatched cameras are unsuitable for numeric image comparisons. Report crop or projective alignment if making a matched image comparison. Raw occupancy, opacity distributions and counts can flag anomalies for review. Avoid promoting a result solely because those numbers moved.

## Technical and run gates

- Keep existing frame, registration, result-size, and artifact validity checks. Open the actual SPZ in the intended SparkJS viewer.
- Record tool and profile versions, input and output hashes, frame sizes, stage times, output MiB, and time to the first usable view in the tested setup. State whether upload, queue, network or software rendering was involved.
- Verify an unchanged project baseline and applicable code changes with `npm run test:unit`, `npm run build`, `npm run test:browser`, and `node scripts/verify-scan.mjs`. The checkout previously passed 26 marketplace and 15 simulated scan checks. The Linux baseline had 196/197 unit tests with one Windows path assumption. Establish the actual result on the Helios.
- Verify that normal scan behavior remains available, the earlier prepared Sora flow stays separate, and the candidate can be selected explicitly using a documented opt-in. Preserve previous job compatibility. A replay, fixture, and live scan must have distinct provenance.
- Treat phone viewport emulation, local file replay, the physical phone camera, and a phone viewing a real result over HTTPS as different checks. Leave any unobserved physical behavior clearly pending.
- Deliver a demonstrated visual winner only if it passes the visual gate. Otherwise keep the strongest observed pair for a follow-up and record what still fails.

## Timing after the quality gate

Track capture-to-preview delay and worker-stage latency separately. Attribute any speed improvement to the tested modification. Check that comparisons have the same source, hardware, load, and output quality floor. Keep exact numbers and avoid multiplier claims unsupported by measured runs.
```

### ZIP snapshot MORNING_TEMPLATE.md

```text
# Morning handoff

**Result** — Promoted opt-in candidate / best diagnostic result only / blocked.

**Open first** — Absolute path or loopback URL and the exact command to start it.

**Why this result** — One brief evidence-backed verdict and a link to the visual comparison.

## Where the work lives

| Item | Location |
|---|---|
| Original checkout | |
| Implementation worktree and branch | |
| Baseline and final commit | |
| Run directory and ledger | |
| Opt-in command and output | |
| Matched comparison and orbit | |
| Benchmark and test reports | |

## Results

| Input and role | Compared versions | Visual verdict | Worker time | First usable view | MiB | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Golden, tuning | | | | | | |
| Repeat, confirmation | | | | | | |
| 10.03-second take, stress | | | | | | |

## Changes and verification

- State which code changes were kept, where, and why.
- List exact commands run and passed or failed counts. Include the environment and separate simulated, replay, and real hardware checks.
- Call out any candidate rejected because it removed useful detail or appeared to work only from one angle.
- State which test runs or measurements hit the deadline, failed, or are still unobserved.
- Record whether the result can be reproduced from the original clip and which profile or masks are required.

## First physical phone test

Use the intended phone, rear camera, browser, laptop and venue or fallback network. Record permission, actual camera resolution, upload, real processing, viewer first usable view, orbit, reload, and handling of a genuine failure. Keep the browser recording separate from an earlier local replay in the report.
```

### ZIP snapshot AUDIT.md

```text
# Chair scan evidence audit

**Scope:** LihatDulu `main` at `a26d952` and the seven-take evidence branch at `cbf69f09fbb41eacaa8ea40e6e6cb55ceec61e4c`. The source originals and aligned frame sets remain on the Helios. This audit reviewed all published previews and stills, decoded the seven SPZs, inspected repository code, and ran an isolated browser diagnostic on Golden and the 10.03-second variant. It did not retrain or alter the production repository.

**Verdict:** All seven published opening views fail as furniture inspection views. Better camera framing shows more of the chair in some cases, but a trial that targeted oversized splats left substantial room debris and distortion. The work should attack both viewer framing and reconstruction quality.

## What the evidence says

All 80 manifest assets were present. One Markdown digest matched after restoring Windows line endings. The seven original SPZs decoded successfully, with finite position and scale columns and splat counts matching the manifest. The published stills are frames 0, middle, and last, so they show source appearance and visible room clutter but cannot establish full orbit coverage. The published mobile screenshots are desktop-browser emulations.

| Take | Frames registered | Worker stages | SPZ MiB | Full vs percentile box diagonal | Max vs median longest splat axis |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Golden, 25.35 s | 76/76 | 4:51 | 0.52 | 1.89× | 95.8× |
| Repeat, 20.13 s | 60/60 | 4:50 | 0.47 | 2.25× | 179.0× |
| 10.03 s | 60/60 | 5:33 | 0.68 | 2.83× | 115.6× |
| 10.21 s | 60/60 | 4:04 | 0.50 | 2.22× | 131.0× |
| 14.59 s | 72/72 | 4:19 | 0.51 | 2.45× | 202.9× |
| 15.00 s, extraction retry | 74/74 | 4:30 | 0.62 | 2.26× | 131.0× |
| 4K-source 14.62 s | 71/71 | 4:05 | 0.34 | 1.54× | 70.1× |

The percentile box uses per-axis 1st and 99th percentiles of splat centers. It measures outlier sensitivity and is not a semantic chair boundary. Longest-axis scale is the maximum of each decoded splat's three linear scale estimates. Neither diagnostic identifies which splats belong to the chair. See [the raw diagnostic stats](evidence/splat-stats.json), [all seven published views and stills](evidence/published-views.jpg), and the [reframing and trim comparison](evidence/reframe-and-trim.jpg).

Golden's published view has bright haze over the chair. Repeat and the 10.03-second variant show small fragments. The 10.21-second and 14.59-second takes also open with small streaked views. The 15-second and 4K-source takes show more colored material without providing a clear chair silhouette at opening. Registration, splat counts, and a centered PNG all failed as visual proxies here.

## Findings that change the run order

| Finding | Evidence | Overnight implication |
|---|---|---|
| **Viewer centers in the wrong coordinate system.** | `src/scan-viewer.js` applies a rotation to the model, then subtracts its unrotated local center. A synthetic nonzero-center test reproduced the displacement. A corrected render moved the real Golden model, with haze still present. | Fix the transform independently of training. Capture the same file before and after. |
| **The view fits the whole reconstruction.** | `getBoundingBox(true)` fits all splat centers, including room points and outliers. All seven full diagonals are larger than their percentile boxes. Reframing made the Golden detail larger without producing a usable chair. Default camera controls move through the scene instead of maintaining a chair-centered orbit. | Use stable object framing, orbit, reset and resize. Verify actual views and inspect the rotation convention before deriving a pose. |
| **The artifacts contain large, distracting splats and room content.** | The longest splat axes vary sharply. In a controlled diagnostic copy, dropping about the largest 1% removed 270 splats from Golden and 317 from the 10.03-second take. It reduced some haze and streaks. A Golden view turned 90° was still dominated by a cupboard, and the short take still included floor and background fragments. | Treat cheap cleanup as an experiment. Move to object-aware training and capture coverage if the result remains unusable. Protect thin chair details during filtering. |
| **The reported frame size is wrong for this footage.** | `server/scan-pipeline.mjs` uses `scale=1280:-2`, which fixes width. All 21 published stills are portrait **1280 × 2276**, even though the metadata reports encoded landscape dimensions and the handoff says 1280 long edge. FFmpeg preserves aspect ratio for the computed negative dimension. | Measure encoded and displayed orientation separately. Confirm actual dataset dimensions. For a speed candidate use a real long-edge cap and rerun alignment on resized frames. Treat the older dimension claim as unverified for the full dataset. |
| **Existing checks measure process completion.** | The harness verifies ready state, a splat count, and PNG size. The earlier verdict used center/edge metrics. Seven visually weak defaults passed. | Separate processing complete, rendered, visually usable, and physical phone verified. Add fixed-view and actual image review gates. |
| **Capture advice combines tasks.** | The timer's prompts move from an orbit to open drawers and defect close-ups in one take. The study's baseline recommends a complete, overlapping orbit. The published stills include partial framing and room clutter. | Keep the main geometry pass consistent and validate coverage from the actual aligned frame set. Keep any separate detail evidence out of the geometry experiment. |
| **Replay and timing provenance can mislead.** | Batch titles are filenames and dimensions are fixture values. The result page uses fresh-recording language. Worker total sums four stages and excludes capture, transfer, queue, and viewer load. | Label replay accurately, preserve real and provisional metadata, and time replay-to-usable-view separately. Do not infer dimensions from the SPZ. |
| **Local trial isolation matters.** | The scan server prunes completed jobs at startup and during runs. The worker deletes its temporary aligned dataset after success. Tracked `dist/` predates the scan feature. | Use isolated jobs, preserve reusable alignment before cleanup, and build from the implementation worktree. |

The experimental trim wrote a separate SPZ. Its re-encoding changes the byte hash, so treat the comparison as diagnostic and include a verified no-op round-trip render in future A/Bs. The first attempt to write a trim used an incorrect argument position and produced a seven-splat file. It was rejected by structural verification and replaced before review.

## Speed and remaining proof

Across the seven lab runs, alignment consumed about **45%** and Brush training **54%** of summed worker time. SPZ conversion took about **0.52 s** at the median. The fresh build's lazy scan-viewer bundle was about **959 kB gzip** before loading an asset. That makes alignment and training the first worker speed targets after quality passes, with viewer transfer and first usable view measured separately.

The previous fresh build passed 26 marketplace and 15 simulated scan checks. Unit tests were 196/197 in the Linux environment because one Brush argument test passes Windows-style paths to the native path module. Those checks establish workflow behavior, and the lab study establishes laptop reconstruction. A new capture from the physical phone over HTTPS and end-to-end phone timing are still open.

## References

- [Pinned evidence and study manifest](https://github.com/Fafauzif/Lihatdulu/blob/cbf69f09fbb41eacaa8ea40e6e6cb55ceec61e4c/docs/handoff/scan-evidence-manifest.json)
- [Viewer on the audited baseline](https://github.com/Fafauzif/Lihatdulu/blob/a26d952/src/scan-viewer.js) and [worker on the audited baseline](https://github.com/Fafauzif/Lihatdulu/blob/a26d952/server/scan-pipeline.mjs)
- [Lab report](https://github.com/Fafauzif/Lihatdulu/blob/a26d952/docs/handoff/scan-toolchain.md)
- [FFmpeg scale filter](https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg-filters.html#scale-1), [Spark camera controls](https://sparkjs.dev/docs/controls/), and [Brush release notes for mask support](https://github.com/ArthurBrussee/brush/releases). Check the locally pinned help before running version-specific options.
```

### ZIP snapshot tools/preflight.mjs

```javascript
import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
import { createReadStream } from "node:fs";
import { readFile, stat, statfs } from "node:fs/promises";
import { delimiter, dirname, isAbsolute, join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const directory = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
const lock = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(directory, "../evidence/lock.json"), "utf8"));
const usage = "node overnight-scan/tools/preflight.mjs [--root path] [--clips path] [--evidence path] [--json]";
const values = {};
const argumentsList = process.argv.slice(2);
for (let index = 0; index < argumentsList.length; index++) {
  const arg = argumentsList[index];
  if (arg === "--help") {
    console.log(usage);
    process.exit(0);
  }
  if (arg === "--json") {
    values.json = true;
    continue;
  }
  if (!["--root", "--clips", "--evidence"].includes(arg) || !argumentsList[index + 1] || argumentsList[index + 1].startsWith("--")) {
    console.error(usage);
    process.exit(2);
  }
  values[arg.slice(2)] = argumentsList[++index];
}

const root = resolve(values.root ?? join(directory, "../.."));
const clips = resolve(values.clips ?? join(root, lock.captureDirectory));
const evidence = resolve(values.evidence ?? root);
const windows = process.platform === "win32";

async function present(path) {
  try {
    return (await stat(path)).isFile();
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

async function sha256(path) {
  const hash = createHash("sha256");
  for await (const chunk of createReadStream(path)) hash.update(chunk);
  return hash.digest("hex");
}

function git(...args) {
  const result = spawnSync("git", ["-C", root, ...args], { encoding: "utf8", windowsHide: true, timeout: 5000 });
  return result.status === 0 ? result.stdout.trim() : null;
}

async function findTool(environmentVariable, basename, defaultPath) {
  const executable = basename + (windows ? ".exe" : "");
  const override = process.env[environmentVariable];
  const locations = [];
  if (override && isAbsolute(override)) {
    locations.push(override);
  }
  if (defaultPath) {
    locations.push(join(root, defaultPath));
  }
  for (const folder of (process.env.PATH ?? "").split(delimiter).filter(Boolean)) {
    locations.push(join(folder, override && !isAbsolute(override) ? override : executable));
  }
  for (const path of locations) {
    if (await present(path)) return path;
  }
  return null;
}

if (!await present(join(root, "package.json")) || git("rev-parse", "--is-inside-work-tree") !== "true") {
  console.error("LihatDulu checkout not found. Extract at the repo root or pass --root.");
  process.exit(2);
}
const takes = await Promise.all(lock.takes.map(async (take) => ({
  name: take.take,
  role: take.role,
  clip: join(clips, take.clip),
  clipExists: await present(join(clips, take.clip)),
  evidence: await Promise.all(take.files.map(async (file) => {
    const location = join(evidence, file.path);
    if (!await present(location)) return { path: file.path, status: "missing" };
    const bytes = (await stat(location)).size;
    const digest = await sha256(location);
    return { path: file.path, status: bytes === file.bytes && digest === file.sha256 ? "match" : "mismatch" };
  })),
})));

const available = await statfs(root, { bigint: true }).then((fileSystem) =>
  Number(fileSystem.bavail * fileSystem.bsize) / (1024 ** 3)).catch(() => null);
const [ffmpeg, ffprobe, colmap, brush] = await Promise.all([
  findTool("SCAN_FFMPEG", "ffmpeg"),
  findTool("SCAN_FFPROBE", "ffprobe"),
  findTool("SCAN_COLMAP", "colmap", "var/tools/colmap/bin/colmap.exe"),
  findTool("SCAN_BRUSH", "brush", "var/tools/brush/brush_app.exe"),
]);
const result = {
  root,
  clips,
  evidence,
  node: process.version,
  branch: git("branch", "--show-current") || "(detached)",
  head: git("rev-parse", "HEAD"),
  originMain: git("rev-parse", "--verify", "origin/main"),
  fetchedEvidence: git("rev-parse", "--verify", "origin/" + lock.evidenceBranch),
  expectedEvidence: lock.evidenceCommit,
  workingTree: git("status", "--short"),
  freeGiB: available === null ? null : Number(available.toFixed(1)),
  toolCandidates: { ffmpeg, ffprobe, colmap, brush },
  takes,
};

if (values.json) {
  console.log(JSON.stringify(result, null, 2));
} else {
  console.log("LihatDulu preflight");
  console.log("Checkout", result.root);
  console.log("Node / branch / HEAD", result.node, result.branch, result.head);
  console.log("Working tree", result.workingTree === "" ? "clean" : result.workingTree);
  console.log("Free GiB", result.freeGiB ?? "unknown");
  console.log("Original clips", result.clips);
  console.log("Evidence", result.evidence, result.fetchedEvidence === result.expectedEvidence ? "(pinned ref fetched)" : "(check pinned commit)");
  for (const [tool, path] of Object.entries(result.toolCandidates)) console.log(tool, path ?? "not found");
  for (const take of result.takes) {
    const counts = take.evidence.reduce((acc, item) => ({ ...acc, [item.status]: (acc[item.status] ?? 0) + 1 }), {});
    console.log(take.name, take.clipExists ? "clip present" : "clip missing", JSON.stringify(counts));
  }
  console.log("Tool candidates are paths. The runbook checks versions and executes capability checks before use.");
}
```

## Original context pack archive

The following is the full, unedited 16 October 2026 V8.0 master pack. The November edition above governs current status. The first line after the marker begins the original file. The complete original tail is 334,842 bytes with SHA-256 `1570e2a961c95b27fd485d17b5adcfd7e7eca365cbcbda72c1fcc52c7e000779`.

<!-- BEGIN ORIGINAL V8.0 PACK -->
# LihatDulu: Master AI Context Pack — V8.0

**Current release:** 8.0.0  
**Updated:** 16 October 2026, Asia/Jakarta  
**Current source export:** `LihatDulu_V8_Vercel.zip`  
**Status:** implemented, built, verified and delivered as a Vercel-ready source archive; no V8 live deployment was performed in this work.

## V8 — Read this first

This V8 section is the active continuation record. It supersedes conflicting V5.1/V5/V4/V3 statements in the historical archive below, including statements that call themselves “current”, require one active cabinet, default every product to 3D, demand physical camera capture, or say to wait for old unanswered questions. The complete supplied previous master pack is preserved byte-for-byte between the V8 archive markers for provenance. It is history, not a second active brief.

The user asked to implement the supplied marketplace audit/redesign handoff and return a deployable Vercel ZIP. That work is complete. The latest request is to update this master context pack. Do not infer authorization for a new hosting provider, a live deployment, real transactions or backend expansion from this documentation update.

**Authority:** latest explicit user direction → actual supplied source/assets → fresh applicable verification → this V8 record → the 16 October audit's remaining proposals → historical archives. If a later source package differs, inspect it and reconcile the differences before editing. Do not use dated QA counts to certify new code.

## V8 — Release identity and source authority

| Item | Current record |
| --- | --- |
| Project | LihatDulu, Indonesian furniture resale marketplace concept |
| Main audience | Shoppers exploring the concept; competition judges and potential investors |
| Interface language | Natural Indonesian; IDR and centimetres formatted with `id-ID`; saved-record times in `Asia/Jakarta` |
| Implementation | Vite, plain JavaScript modules, CSS, Three.js; retained stack, no framework migration |
| Release version | `8.0.0` in root/app manifests and lockfiles |

Read the release documents and actual import graph before relying on old module names. `docs/V7_REFINEMENT.md` and older scripts are retained historical context. The uploaded audit supplied the implementation direction; a proposal inside it is not proof that every optional feature shipped.

The original input was `LihatDulu_V7_1_Vercel.zip`, SHA-256 `95c3aee45764c794ef1eaa689a37b838fe079bcddd1fd89cdce90bf899f04091`. Its manifests said `7.0.1`; V8 corrected release-label consistency. The audit is `LihatDulu_Marketplace_Deep_Audit_and_Redesign_Handoff.md`, SHA-256 `f5459e2ac75d7adf0a947e103ec4c5836567a8a4443d661dc556ad229218836b`. It includes detailed contracts and an Etsy reference analysis. The reference screenshots are static design evidence; no live Etsy behavior or backend was established by that audit.

## V8 — Product scope and preserved decisions

LihatDulu now presents a product-led furniture marketplace: visible asking prices, size, condition, seller/city and example acquisition costs support the distinctive inspection and room tools. Keep the restrained forest-green/sage identity and bundled Plus Jakarta Sans. The root is a compact buyer/seller entrance with real priced example inventory, not an inspection-only redirect or a large introductory funnel.

Preserve these boundaries unless a new user brief explicitly changes them:

- Furniture only: ten active authored examples and two coming-soon records.
- Four original GLBs, canonical closed dimensions, genuine model interaction and geometric motion/room calculations.
- Sora-only prepared seller video; useful partial evidence and buyer preview.
- Frontend-only, bounded browser-local state. No account or cross-device synchronization.
- No real payment, order submission, reservation, seller contact, chat delivery, authentication, upload, device camera/microphone/display acquisition, reconstruction or live image inference.
- No fabricated stars, reviews, sales, verification, response times, shipping promises, discounts, demand or stock activity.
- Example data and local outcomes disclosed near relevant decisions, without a large prototype banner on every screen.
- Source ZIP delivery with prebuilt output and Vercel instructions. A preview link alone is not an equivalent deliverable.

Retained historical film/reference/TTS material remains in the archive. V8 website work did not create a new film, play all historical videos, or establish a working reconstruction service.

## V8 — What changed from V7.1

| Area | Implemented V8 behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Home/catalogue | Compact opening, earlier priced inventory, one clear global search, three real parent groups, no repeated large marketing hero |
| Shared cards | Image → title → asking price → dimensions/condition → stable seller/city → media/save/compare controls; no tall fixed body requirement |
| Filters | Shared draft/Apply/Cancel/reset contract; whole-rupiah validation; accurate result/preview totals; decorative histogram/slider removed |
| Task continuity | Scoped no-result recovery, preserved query/sort, bounded history context and synchronous Back scroll restoration |
| Product detail | Gallery-first normal entry; direct 3D/room/condition URLs retained; buying facts and total exposed earlier |
| Costs/completion | Shared fee selector on PDP/cart/review; distinct read-only review and reopenable immutable local summaries |
| Offers | Visible local draft lifecycle, legacy migration, formatted amount preview, note retention, current/reference price distinction, delete/Undo and truthful storage status |
| Saved workspace | Items, collections, offer drafts and summaries; true collection detail/membership and explicit comparison selection |
| Evidence | Card covers separated from exact gallery originals; accurate media types, three specific Sora notes and named detail reader |
| Sellers | Stable seller IDs, legacy store aliases, honest one-item inventories, separately labelled other-seller alternatives |
| Prepared references | Reference and results first; authored source-specific order; exact width filtering; both reset paths retain the reference |
| Viewer resilience | Useful initial-load and runtime context-loss fallback; retry; rendering suspension offscreen/when hidden; cleanup on navigation |
| Shared interaction | Named native dialogs, explicit Tab containment/return focus, dirty-offer close policy, coordinated fixed surfaces and zoom reflow |
| Delivery | Consolidated shared CSS, consistent version 8.0.0, 16 route metadata pages, explicit noindex policy, verified source ZIP |

Optional question notes, saved shops, recently viewed history, live messaging/reviews and production commerce were not added merely because the audit discussed them. Do not describe them as shipped.

## V8 — Discovery state and return behavior

The URL owns applied catalogue state; the form owns an uncommitted draft. Apply a changed valid draft once. Applying unchanged state adds no duplicate history entry. Cancel, close and Escape discard filter edits. Reset refinements clears categories/price/dimensions/location/material/condition/3D in the draft while preserving query and sort. Hapus filter commits that same scoped reset. One chip removes only its criterion.

Use OR within each categorical group and AND between groups. The active badge counts seven possible groups: category, price, dimensions, city, material, condition, 3D; query/sort do not count. Multiple cities count as one group. Per-option counts are omitted; current result totals and valid Apply preview are accurate. Empty bounds mean unlimited. Invalid min/max does not become a valid zero-result preview. The removed price graph was decorative; do not restore it without deriving it from real catalogue data.

Search on `/beli` replaces `q` while preserving its visible refinements/sort. Global search from another route starts a fresh catalogue query and does not inherit product tabs, seller state or prepared reference parameters. The app-owned clear button removes q and returns input focus. Composition is guarded for IME input. Search is local and submitted explicitly, not a remote matching service.

Sort values: `relevance` means canonical editorial order labelled “Pilihan LihatDulu”; `newest` uses supplied record dates; `price-asc` and `price-desc` have deterministic stable-ID ties. Ascending starts Ruji Rp780,000, Tala Rp950,000. Descending starts Rimba Rp3,150,000, Awan Rp2,850,000.

History stores bounded scrollY and opened listing ID alongside the URL. Back restores results **synchronously after render**. This fixes a warm-cache case where requestAnimationFrame-only restoration exposed the top of the page before the next frame. Final testing restored exactly 1,494px on three consecutive Ruji trips with three compared items.

## V8 — Costs, local offers, collections and summaries

### One pricing selector

`market-state.js::checkoutTotals` owns current eligible item prices, stable seller grouping, preparation and selected fulfilment. Display names do not determine seller identity or fees. The same selector supplies PDP, cart, review and new snapshot creation. One unit per used item; no quantity inflation.

| Seller preparation example | Fee IDR |
| --- | ---: |
| studio-sora | 150,000 |
| ruang-kedua | 125,000 |
| atelier-batu | 100,000 |
| rimba-objects | 100,000 |
| Other current seller IDs | 100,000 default |

Preparation and delivery are charged once per seller group, not once per cart line. Pickup delivery fee is zero. Example delivery adds Rp150,000 per seller. These are disclosed example amounts, not an operational service or carrier quotation. Do not invent what the preparation service covers.

| Sora cost | Pickup | Example delivery |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Item | Rp2,450,000 | Rp2,450,000 |
| Preparation | Rp150,000 | Rp150,000 |
| Delivery | Rp0 | Rp150,000 |
| Total | **Rp2,600,000** | **Rp2,750,000** |

UI currency uses Indonesian separators. Selecting delivery updates both the detailed panel and mobile total. A saved offer does not replace the cart asking price.

### Offer lifecycle

One validated local draft per listing: schema, listingId, amountIdr, note, referencePriceIdr, createdAt, updatedAt and optional migratedAt. An empty composer amount avoids implying a seller-approved suggested bid. Valid new saves require a whole rupiah amount from Rp1 through the **current** asking price and a note of at most 500 characters.

The offer parser accepts plain digits or Indonesian dot-grouped thousands (optional Rp prefix); it rejects decimals/comma formats. Filter-price parsing is a separate helper and also supports consistently comma-grouped thousands. Follow each existing helper and field hint rather than silently changing number interpretation.

Composer identifies item, seller/city, one-item basis, current/reference prices, local-only result, formatted amount preview and note count. Invalid input stays editable with visible associated errors. Enter in notes stays multiline. Dirty close/Escape offers keep-editing or deliberate discard; untouched cancellation is simple.

Saving exposes a persistent product action and saved-workspace record. Delete has Undo. Old `lihatdulu:offer:<id>` amount/note records migrate without invented original timestamps/reference prices. A later lower asking price or unavailable status does not erase the old amount/note; show the difference and require valid revision before a new save. Nothing has been sent or accepted.

### Saved collections and snapshots

Collections are copies of a saved set, with actual item detail and add/remove membership controls. Same-name overwrite needs an explicit second confirmation. More than three members opens a deliberate comparison selector; no silent truncation. Reversible cart, collection/member, offer and summary removals expose Undo.

Review is a distinct read-only step with an edit path back to cart. Saving produces a validated **immutable** snapshot containing item facts, sellers, dimensions, images, asking prices, fulfilment, group fees, totals and saved time. Keep at most ten snapshots. Reopening after normal refresh reads that snapshot; later cart edits, price changes or unavailability must not rewrite history. Show current changes separately. No real order number/payment/seller message is implied.

Persistence outcomes are `device-persistent`, `temporary-in-tab`, or `failed`. Storage denial/quota fallback uses bounded memory with a visible session-only notice; reload may remove it. Failed saves/validation retain the user's typed copy. Do not report a durable save after swallowing a storage exception.

## V8 — Models, imagery and condition evidence

Preserve all four original GLBs. Hashes below were checked against the delivered package; they protect identity, not a certification of physical furniture.

| Model file under `app/public/assets/` | Bytes | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| cabinet.glb | 4,702,852 | `18cf94f5d342f9949d5b100bde3968dfc14b1746c3cb47570cf930ab55c9d742` |
| arca.glb | 15,468 | `376a875a4f7fe6af102d89292d5a1abb1eca1eaa7ea93d80c61678ae0c3afe28` |
| batu.glb | 24,452 | `45d556bfb599f4a3e7c4c3668acdb7e71241e540dbb15683225c5642e0debe60` |
| rimba.glb | 29,780 | `7881860eb17b19f25943f3d5ebb36f4c3719556a19c6bf8e505b0ced686719d9` |

Models use metres and the established X-width/Y-up/+Z-front convention. Preserve actual closed bounds, pivots, room/obstacle checks and motion limits. Sora remains two independent doors and one drawer; never make a CSS image transform stand in for real 3D. Use source/manifest/geometry tests for product-specific kinematics rather than importing old assumptions into another model.

`productCover` resolves 800×800 edited transparent catalogue covers. `components.image` and `mediaSrc` preserve explicit sources. `galleryMedia` describes original renders, supplied example photos and prepared condition details. Do not globally substitute a cover whenever its first-gallery filename appears. Model-derived images are labelled as renders; a one-photo item has one image, no fictional alternate views or model controls.

| Sora note ID | Known evidence | Exact source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| scratch | Left-door surface scratch, 11cm long | `/images/wear-scratch.webp` |
| chip | Upper-right corner chip, 8 × 5mm | `/images/wear-chip.webp` |
| mark | Top surface ring mark, 5.5cm diameter | `/images/wear-mark.webp` |

Three unique condition notes, seven Sora gallery assets and zero-to-six selected seller groups are separate counts. A 3D capability is not another evidence note. Other records state when condition close-ups are unavailable. No load-strength, odor, pest, age or structural certification may be inferred from the images.

Photo-first routes do not load GLBs or the optional engine/GLTFLoader bundles. Explicit model/room entries do. Loading/failure retains a matching image, facts, gallery route and retry; unavailable controls do not imply success. Runtime context loss clears the failed engine and shows the same recovery route. Intersection/document visibility pauses expensive rendering offscreen/hidden; returning resumes. Leaving the route disposes mounts/listeners/graphics resources. Successful software WebGL is not a guarantee for every GPU.

## V8 — Prepared seller and reference journeys

Seller uses the real **18-second prepared Sora clip**, six 3-second areas: `front`, `right`, `rear`, `interior`, `drawer`, `scratch`. Playback time and selected evidence are separate. End-of-chapter/manual selection records unique groups; retake replaces its group's record without increasing the unique count. Partial review remains useful. Restart playback preserves evidence; empty-evidence reset is separate and reversible. Exit pauses/persists, and return requires explicit playback. Buyer preview reflects local selections, disables purchase/offer and returns to review. A preview URL does not transfer local state to another browser. No recording, reconstruction, publication or sharing occurred.

Prepared discovery at `/cari-dari-foto` owns only `source` and `width`, not catalogue q/sort. Authored order is Arca → Tala → Senja for source Arca and Tala → Arca → Senja for source Tala. Reference, width limit and ordinary priced result cards come first. An optional expanded 12-second movement scene uses **Arca even when Tala is selected**, and says so. It is not camera acquisition or live similarity inference.

| Width limit with source Tala | Actual eligible results |
| --- | --- |
| 50cm | None |
| 60cm | Tala |
| 72cm | Tala, Senja |
| 72.6cm or 73cm | Tala, Arca, Senja in authored source order |
| Empty | All three |

Both reset-size paths remove width only and retain source. Width validation accepts supported 1–500cm numeric values; invalid entry retains the field/URL and displays an associated error. Invalid shared source/width normalize to safe supported state. Scene/WebGL failure leaves useful reference/results available.

## V8 — Design, accessibility and fixed surfaces

Runtime token ownership is `app/src/brand-tokens.css`; `DESIGN.md` mirrors accepted values and explains intent. Preserve local Plus Jakarta Sans throughout, tabular price/dimension numbers, quiet borders and restrained motion.

| Role | Current value |
| --- | --- |
| Canvas | #F6FBF1 |
| Primary / hover | #00291D / #173F32 |
| Surface / subtle | #FFFFFF / #EDF2E8 |
| Media stage | #EEF1E7 |
| Main / muted text | #181D17 / #414845 |
| Border | #DCE4D9 |
| Sparse warm accent | #88502F |
| Error / focus | #923D34 / #426BDF |

Desktop max width 1440px, generally 32px gutters; wide catalogue has a 218px rail and four columns, three below 1300px. Filters move to a modal below 1050px. Phones normally show two columns, with available-width auto-fit reflow for extreme zoom. Card corners 12px, controls 9px, dialogs 14px. Primary actions 48px; common secondary targets aim for 44px. Phone input text 16px; essential metadata remains readable rather than squeezed to fit fixed heights.

`stitch.css` now owns consolidated shared shell/card/modal styles. The old `refinement.css` layer is retired; named discovery/product/buyer CSS files own their components. Preserve visible global scrollbar tokens, standards properties and engine fallbacks. Product desktop gallery is capped around 540px; condition preview can expand from a concise summary; purchase facts/cost/actions precede long description.

Use semantic links/buttons, named `showModal()` dialogs, explicit Tab/Shift+Tab containment, Escape and return focus. Forms use `novalidate` with app-owned visible errors, aria association and first-invalid focus. Native select popup ownership is intentional. Toasts supplement persistent records; they are not the only outcome. Respect reduced motion and do not reintroduce entrance animation that hides inventory.

Product phones use one purchase bar and suppress bottom navigation. Other phone pages use navigation; catalogue compare tray sits above it. Tablet/desktop comparison also reserves footer space. Active dialogs suppress background nav/purchase/compare bars and keep long forms/last actions reachable. Do not add a third independently fixed bar or blanket page-height clipping.

Current collection store bounds: 20 collections, at most 100 allowed IDs each (actual release has ten active authored IDs). Buyer summaries cap at ten; offer note at 500 characters. Preserve normalization and escaping. Corrupt records recover valid peers where supported; no global clearing of unrelated browser data. Different deployment origins have independent local state; sharing URLs cannot synchronize it. Storage events refresh idle app surfaces while an active composer retains typed work. No shared multi-user conflict service exists.

## V8 — Build, deployment and packaging

Use the extracted project root containing `package.json` and `vercel.json`, **not `app/` as the Vercel Root Directory**. Node is specified as `24.x`; use lockfiles rather than an incidental dependency upgrade.

```sh
# Install/run the app locally from the extracted root.
npm --prefix app ci
npm --prefix app run dev

# Complete Vercel-equivalent build from root.
npm run build

# View prebuilt application locally.
npm --prefix app run preview
```

Root build executes `npm --prefix app ci && npm --prefix app run build && node scripts/prepare-site.mjs`. It creates `app/dist`, generates 16 route metadata pages and copies identical output to root `dist`. Vercel configuration uses framework `vite`, build command `npm run build`, output `app/dist`, known-route metadata rewrites and a remaining SPA fallback.

Known generated routes: `/`, `/beli`, `/jual`, `/cari-dari-foto` and all twelve `/barang/:id` records. Metadata includes title/description, Open Graph, canonical and Twitter card data. `SITE_URL` can set the origin; otherwise build-time Vercel project/preview environment URLs are used, then the reference origin for local builds. The concept is intentionally **noindex, nofollow** in HTML and response headers. No fabricated rating/live-stock structured data is present. Actual messaging-app unfurls remain unverified.

Vercel CLI instructions in the release are `npx vercel` for a preview and, after reviewing that concrete preview, `npx vercel --prod` for publication. No API key, backend, paid service or new project/account is required for local operation. This is a deployment handoff, not proof of a V8 deployment. Do not claim any old hosted URL is updated without a real receipt/check for that deployment.

The delivered ZIP includes editable source, tests, required local assets/font, design/flow/QA/deployment docs, both build trees and the file manifest. It excludes node_modules, credentials, VCS internals, transient QA output, private browser profiles and review transcripts. CRC and every manifest payload hash were checked. The application ZIP was not repacked merely to update this standalone master document.

## V8 — Verification and honest limits

**Evidence is dated 16 October 2026 and applies to the delivered V8 source, not to future changes.** This documentation update rechecked source facts, package identity and the saved reports; it did not rerun the entire application suite.

| Check | Actual V8 result |
| --- | --- |
| Unit suite | 127/127 passed; includes retained model hashes, geometry/motion, state validation, discovery, media, fees and buyer-record contracts |
| Build | Passed; consistent 8.0.0 manifests; 16 metadata pages; identical static output trees |
| Release verifier | Passed; ten complete 800×800 alpha covers and referenced built assets/imports |
| Retained production suite | Passed 13 deep routes and 99 byte-identical copied public assets; real Sora model/Arca room render and 18-second video |
| Full V8 acceptance run | **25/26 groups passed**, with one Back-position failure; do not relabel this report as a pristine 26/26 run |
| Final targeted rerun after fixing Back | **2/2 groups passed**: three consecutive warm-cache returns restored exactly 1,494px with three compared items; no error-boundary issues |
| Responsive layout | 77 route/viewport pairs passed, plus 200% CSS zoom and long-name fixture, 79 layout records total |
| Runtime guard observations | Zero page errors, unexpected console errors, failed assets, external requests or device-media calls in the cited final checks |
| Design document lint | Zero errors/warnings |
| Maintained-source formatting | Prettier checks passed |
| Packaged source ZIP | 441 files; CRC and payload hashes passed; SHA-256 recorded above |
| Strict premium static auditor | Regex-parser timeout; **not a passing audit or accessibility certification** |

All acceptance groups have applicable passing coverage when the final corrected Back test is combined with the rest of the full run. This is cumulative evidence across the full run and a targeted rerun; it is not a claim that the full 26-group run was executed again after the last fix.

Layout viewports: 320×568, 390×844, 430×932, 768×1024, 1024×768, 1280×800, 1440×900. Covered buying/local completion, offers/collections/Undo/migration, storage denial, unavailable records, source-aware media/evidence, reference width resets, dialogs, fixed surfaces, seller playback and actual model failure/context-loss/offscreen recovery. A text-only long-name fixture did not create duplicate shipped inventory.

The final Back fix replaced requestAnimationFrame-only scroll restoration with same-task restoration after render. Gallery counter/enlarge CSS, unavailable saved-card semantics and extreme-zoom grid/sort reflow were also corrected during verification. Do not reintroduce the superseded patterns.

Saved evidence in the working session was `qa-v8-release/report.json` (full run) and `qa-v8-history-final/report.json` (final targeted rerun). These reports/screenshots remain outside the release and are not guaranteed to survive a new conversation. `QA.md` and reproducible source tests travel in the ZIP. If those scratch reports are unavailable, retain the dated result summary and rerun appropriate checks before new claims.

Measured unthrottled local Chromium/SwiftShader profile: normal catalogue/photo/reference routes requested no optional GLB/engine/GLTFLoader; explicit Sora model loaded its retained 4,702,852-byte GLB. Offscreen output fell from 72 frames over 1.2 seconds to zero, then resumed on return. Real context loss led to poster/error, disabled controls and cleared engine; retry restored a mesh render. Those are local byte/frame observations, not Core Web Vitals, field conversion or universal GPU scores.

**Still unverified:** physical iOS/Android keyboard/rotation/safe areas, native Safari, real device/GPU differences, a dedicated screen-reader session, messaging-app unfurls and field performance. Software WebGL and CSS zoom are approximations. Real payments/accounts/messages/delivery/reconstruction/visual retrieval remain outside implemented scope, not hidden “almost complete” features.

Reproducible commands from extracted root:

```sh
npm --prefix app run test:unit
npm run build
node scripts/verify-release.mjs
npm --prefix app run test:browser
npm --prefix app run test:production
```

`test:browser`/`test:redesign` use `verify-v8.mjs`. `test:legacy` runs the old V7 browser assertions and is not V8 acceptance; old hero/summary expectations may differ intentionally. The browser helper supports `CHROMIUM_PATH`; Playwright Chromium may need installation on another operating system. Log real command outcomes and inspect reports rather than inferring passes from a script's name.

## V8 — Continuation instructions

For the next implementation session, attach this pack, the current source ZIP and the audit if further design detail is needed. Do not assume this session's scratch paths, installed dependencies, screenshots or hosted preview are available. Use package-relative paths and verify the supplied archive identity.

1. Read `AGENTS.md`, `DESIGN.md`, `FLOW.md`, `UX-CONTRACT.md`, `QA.md`, `README.md`, actual route owners and relevant tests.
2. Reconcile later user changes/source before proposing more work. Preserve retained models, media provenance, catalogue/eligibility, local-only boundaries and price semantics.
3. Use existing shared view/selector/modal/state owners; do not replace this with a framework migration or another cascade patch for one screen.
4. Keep one clear shopping context, truthful example/local status, useful error/empty states and a discoverable outcome for every save.
5. For behavior changes, exercise the affected complete workflow and a sibling screen, phone/tablet/desktop output and relevant failure paths. Preserve original-model/hash/geometry tests and actual rendering checks where affected.
6. Run meaningful unit/build/release/browser checks, record exact full/targeted results and explicit limits. A static audit is not runtime evidence. Do not run expensive unrelated suites only to inflate check totals.
7. For a new source release, update release labels/docs together, rebuild/package with lockfiles, check archive integrity/payloads and return the deployable ZIP. Keep private critic transcripts/scores out of product and release.
8. Record actual deployment URL/version/outcome only if a deployment is explicitly requested and performed. Keep source-export status separate from publication.
9. Update the active front of this pack after durable decisions or implementation facts change, preserving dated history without promoting obsolete instructions.

before changing code. Treat the old master content below the V8 history boundary
as superseded history, even where it calls itself current.

Keep gallery-first normal product entry and direct model/room/condition links.
Do not restore device media acquisition, uploads, real commerce/contact/auth,
reconstruction, fake reviews or invented activity without a new explicit brief.

Use shared discovery/pricing/media/seller/buyer-record owners. Keep applied URL
state distinct from draft filters; preserve scoped resets/search/Back position;
keep all mandatory example costs consistent; expose editable offer drafts and
immutable reopenable summaries with accurate persistence outcomes.

Implement only the user's new request. Verify its complete workflow, relevant
failure cases and responsive output. Record exact tests and limits rather than
copying old pass counts. If a source release is requested, return a validated
Vercel-ready ZIP with editable source, prebuilt output and updated docs. A source
export does not mean any existing site was deployed.

# LihatDulu: Master AI Context Pack

## V5.1 role gateway and marketplace revision

This section is the current authoritative continuation record for the 8 October 2026 role-flow revision. It supersedes any older V5 statement below that says the root route opens directly to Sora. The historical V5 material remains preserved for provenance, but implementation decisions must follow this section and the actual source.

### V5.1 approved decisions

- The root page is a role chooser inspired by the supplied GOPANG reference, while keeping LihatDulu's own warm off-white, forest-green, and terracotta-dot identity.
- The buyer card leads to `/beli`, which presents a commercial-looking product grid with Sora first. Sora is the only active listing and the only card with a real interactive 3D product experience.
- The remaining five cards use the existing local catalogue images as clearly labelled placeholders. They do not invent prices, sellers, stock, reviews, or availability. Selecting any placeholder opens `/barang/sora` so the judge reaches the available inspection experience.
- The seller card leads directly to the existing prepared `/jual` walkthrough. It does not restore camera permissions, upload controls, or a new seller form.
- Existing Sora, room, condition, saved-items, checkout, prepared aliases, and legacy inspection routes remain available. Direct product links continue to open Sora without requiring the role chooser.
- Header, footer, mobile menu, empty states, and seller handoff links use `/beli` or `/barang/sora` according to their meaning. The brand mark remains a route back to the role chooser.

### V5.1 design and QA contract

Use the installed Taste Skill for audit-first, anti-template frontend decisions. Use the bundled marketplace and photography DESIGN.md references as inspiration only, preserving LihatDulu's existing tokens instead of copying another brand. Review changed UI against the Web Interface Guidelines and exercise the new routes with Playwright at phone, tablet, and desktop widths. Verify keyboard focus, real link semantics, image alt text and dimensions, honest placeholder labels, responsive overflow, reduced motion, direct route refreshes, and preservation of the existing 3D fallback, seller walkthrough, room, saved, and checkout journeys.

The updated master pack must record actual verification results. A command description alone is not evidence of a passing check.

### V5.1 verification evidence

Completed 8 October 2026 with Node.js 24, Vite 8.3.2 and Playwright Chromium using software WebGL on Linux. These results are for the final source and rebuilt `dist/` included in this export:

| Unit suite | 57 / 57 passed | State, room, checkout, saved-item and prepared-capture tests |

| Compiled production suite | 22 / 22 passed | Direct route and refresh checks, chooser → marketplace → seller flow, canonical WebGL model, persistence, screenshots and media/upload guards |

The browser checks observed zero camera or file-opening attempts, zero unexpected external requests, no missing assets, no console or page errors, no horizontal overflow, and no source changes during the final evidence run. Screenshots and JSON reports remain under `qa-output/prepared/` and `qa-output/prepared-production/` in the working source when rerunning locally. Native Safari, physical mobile devices and hardware-specific GPU behavior remain outside this verification scope.
